=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
This problem is a harder version of Problem C. Here, the sequence is split into three subarrays.

You are given an integer sequence of length N: A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N).
When splitting A at two positions into three non-empty (contiguous) subarrays, find the maximum possible sum of the counts of distinct integers in those subarrays.
More formally, find the maximum sum of the following three values for a pair of integers (i,j) such that 1 \leq i < j \leq N-1: the count of distinct integers in (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_i), the count of distinct integers in (A_{i+1},A_{i+2},\ldots,A_j), and the count of distinct integers in (A_{j+1},A_{j+2},\ldots,A_{N}).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 3 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq N)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5
3 1 4 1 5

Sample Output 1

5

If we let (i,j) = (2,4) to split the sequence into three subarrays (3,1), (4,1), (5), the counts of distinct integers in those subarrays are 2, 2, 1, respectively, for a total of 5. This sum cannot be greater than 5, so the answer is 5. Other partitions, such as (i,j) = (1,3), (2,3), (3,4), also achieve this sum.

Sample Input 2

10
2 5 6 4 4 1 1 3 1 4

Sample Output 2

9


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Option 1: Recursive segment tree:

class SegTree:
    def __init__(self, arr):
        n=len(arr)
        size=4*n
        self.n=n
        self.max=[0]*size
        self.lazy=[0]*size
        build(1,0,n-1,arr)
    def build...
    def range_add(node,l,r,ql,qr,val)
    def range_max(node,l,r,ql,qr)

N=3e5, recursion function calls maybe okay if using PyPy? AtCoder? Need likely accepted. But let's optimize.

Could implement iterative lazy segment tree (AtCoder style) with size power of two, arrays d (max) and lz (lazy). Range add and range max all_prod? Query prefix. Need implement range_apply(l,r,val) half-open [l,r), prod(l,r) max. This is standard. O(logN) with push/pull. More code but efficient. Let's design.

AtCoder LazySegTree:
- size = 1; log = 0; while size<n: size<<=1; log+=1
- d = [-inf]*(2*size); lz=[0]*size
- set leaves d[size+i]=arr[i]; for i in reversed: d[i]=max(d[2i],d[2i+1])
- all_apply(k,f): d[k]+=f; if k<size: lz[k]+=f
- push(k): if lz[k]!=0: all_apply(2k,lz[k]); all_apply(2k+1,lz[k]); lz[k]=0
- range_apply(l,r,f): if l==r return; l += size; r += size; for i in range(log,0,-1): if ((l>>i)<<i)!=l: push(l>>i); if ((r>>i)<<i)!=r: push((r-1)>>i); then l2=l,r2=r; while l<r: if l&1: all_apply(l,f); l+=1; if r&1: r-=1; all_apply(r,f); l>>=1; r>>=1; then update ancestors: l=l2,r=r2; for i in range(1,log+1): if ((l>>i)<<i)!=l: update(l>>i); if ((r>>i)<<i)!=r: update((r-1)>>i)
- range_prod(l,r): similarly push, then max.

Potential issue with AtCoder lazy segment tree range_apply/prod implementation with n not power of two and -inf leaves. Need ensure update ancestors doesn't overwrite lazy? Standard.

Let's write class LazySegTree:
    def __init__(self, v):
        self._n = len(v)
        self.size = 1
        self.log = 0
        while self.size < self._n:
            self.size <<= 1
            self.log += 1
        self.d = [-10**18] * (2*self.size)
        self.lz = [0] * self.size
        for i in range(self._n):
            self.d[self.size+i] = v[i]
        for i in range(self.size-1,0,-1):
            self.d[i] = self.d[2*i] if self.d[2*i] >= self.d[2*i+1] else self.d[2*i+1]
    def _all_apply(self,k,f):
        self.d[k] += f
        if k < self.size:
            self.lz[k] += f
    def _push(self,k):
        z = self.lz[k]
        if z:
            self._all_apply(2*k,z)
            self._all_apply(2*k+1,z)
            self.lz[k]=0
    def _update(self,k):
        self.d[k] = self.d[2*k] if self.d[2*k] >= self.d[2*k+1] else self.d[2*k+1]
    def range_add(self,l,r,f):
        if l >= r: return
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        l0, r0 = l, r
        for i in range(self.log,0,-1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                self._push(l0 >> i)
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                self._push((r0-1) >> i)
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                self._all_apply(l,f)
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                self._all_apply(r,f)
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        l, r = l0, r0
        for i in range(1,self.log+1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self._update(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self._update((r-1) >> i)
    def range_max(self,l,r):
        if l >= r: return -10**18
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        for i in range(self.log,0,-1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self._push(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self._push((r-1) >> i)
        sml = -10**18
        smr = -10**18
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                if self.d[l] > sml: sml = self.d[l]
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                if self.d[r] > smr: smr = self.d[r]
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        return sml if sml >= smr else smr

Need check range_add push condition. Standard AtCoder:
for i in range(log,0,-1):
    if ((l >> i) << i) != l: push(l >> i)
    if ((r >> i) << i) != r: push((r - 1) >> i)
This pushes ancestors of boundary nodes. Then after apply, update:
for i in range(1, log+1):
    if ((l >> i) << i) != l: update(l >> i)
    if ((r >> i) << i) != r: update((r - 1) >> i)

Let's think about possible bug in iterative lazy range_add update ancestors when l0/r0 are not aligned. Standard AtCoder code uses:
for i in range(1, log+1):
    if ((l >> i) << i) != l: update(l >> i)
    if ((r >> i) << i) != r: update((r - 1) >> i)
where l,r are original l+size, r+size. We use l0,r0. Good.

But in range_add, after push loop, if l0 or r0 aligned, we don't push those ancestors. Then while applies to nodes. Then update loop updates ancestors that may have been modified. Standard.

Let's design final code:

import sys

class LazySegTree:
    __slots__ = ('_n','size','log','d','lz')
    def __init__(self, v):
        self._n = len(v)
        self.size = 1
        self.log = 0
        while self.size < self._n:
            self.size <<= 1
            self.log += 1
        self.d = [-10**18] * (2 * self.size)
        self.lz = [0] * self.size
        for i in range(self._n):
            self.d[self.size + i] = v[i]
        for i in range(self.size - 1, 0, -1):
            self.d[i] = self.d[i << 1] if self.d[i << 1] >= self.d[i << 1 | 1] else self.d[i << 1 | 1]
    def _all_apply(self, k, f):
        self.d[k] += f
        if k < self.size:
            self.lz[k] += f
    def _push(self, k):
        z = self.lz[k]
        if z:
            self._all_apply(k << 1, z)
            self._all_apply(k << 1 | 1, z)
            self.lz[k] = 0
    def _update(self, k):
        self.d[k] = self.d[k << 1] if self.d[k << 1] >= self.d[k << 1 | 1] else self.d[k << 1 | 1]
    def range_add(self, l, r, f):
        if l >= r:
            return
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        l0, r0 = l, r
        for i in range(self.log, 0, -1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                self._push(l0 >> i)
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                self._push((r0 - 1) >> i)
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                self._all_apply(l, f)
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                self._all_apply(r, f)
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        l, r = l0, r0
        for i in range(1, self.log + 1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self._update(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self._update((r - 1) >> i)
    def range_max(self, l, r):
        if l >= r:
            return -10**18
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        for i in range(self.log, 0, -1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self._push(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self._push((r - 1) >> i)
        res = -10**18
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                if self.d[l] > res:
                    res = self.d[l]
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                if self.d[r] > res:
                    res = self.d[r]
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        return res

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]
    # maybe del data? But A references ints; data list can be deleted.
    del data
    last = [0] * (N + 1)
    prev = [0] * (N + 1)
    arr = [0] * (N - 2)
    pref = 0
    for pos, a in enumerate(A, 1):
        p = last[a]
        prev[pos] = p
        if p == 0:
            pref += 1
        if pos <= N - 2:
            arr[pos - 1] = pref
        last[a] = pos
    del last
    S = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for pos in range(N, 0, -1):
        a = A[pos - 1]
        if not seen[a]:
            seen[a] = True
            cnt += 1
        S[pos] = cnt
    del seen, A
    seg = LazySegTree(arr)
    ans = 0
    for j in range(2, N):
        p = prev[j]
        l = 0 if p == 0 else p - 1
        r = j - 1
        seg.range_add(l, r, 1)
        best = seg.range_max(0, r)
        cand = best + S[j + 1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Point_set with lazy:
def set_val(self, p, x):
    p += self.size
    for i in range(self.log, 0, -1):
        self._push(p >> i)
    self.d[p] = x
    for i in range(1, self.log+1):
        self._update(p >> i)
This pushes all ancestors, sets leaf, updates. O(log). Range_add still O(log). Total O(2log) with push in both. Current range_add+range_max also O(2log) with push in both. So no speed improvement. But if we can avoid push in point_set because no lazy on path, point_set O(log) update only. Then total range_add (6log) + point_set (1log) =7log vs current range_add+range_max (6log+4log? Actually range_max has push 2log + while 2log =4log; total10log). So activation with no-push point_set faster. But proving no lazy on path is subtle but true. def set_val(self, p, x):
    p += self.size

        p >>= 1

Can we reduce operations further with a specialized segment tree for range add suffix and prefix max using a Fenwick-like "max" with difference? Let's explore.

We need maintain v_i = P[i] + M(i,j). Updates: for each new A[j]=x, add +1 to v_i for i in [p, j-1] (1-based), where p=prev[j] (or 1 if 0). Query max over i=1..j-1. This is exactly range add on interval [p, j-1] and prefix max. Since intervals always end at current j-1 and start at p (nondecreasing? p can vary). Could use a segment tree with range add and range max. That's what we have.

Can we maintain max with a heap of breakpoints? v_i = P[i] + number of active intervals covering i. Each occurrence adds an interval [p, j-1] to active set? Wait M(i,j) is number of distinct values in [i+1,j]. For a fixed value x, it contributes to M(i,j) for i < last occurrence up to j? Actually for value x, its contribution to M(i,j) is 1 if last occurrence of x up to j is > i. So M(i,j) = number of values x whose last occurrence position L_x(j) > i. Thus v_i = P[i] + count of active values with L_x > i. When j advances to next occurrence of x, L_x updates to j, so for i in [old_L, j-1]? Let's see before update, x's last occurrence was p=prev[j] (or 0 if none). It contributed to M(i,j-1) if p > i (since last occurrence p > i). After update, last becomes j, contributes if j > i. For i >= p, before didn't contribute (p<=i), after contributes (j>i). So +1 for i in [p, j-1]. Same. This is like maintaining a set of intervals (L_x, ∞) and query max of P[i] + count of intervals covering i. Each update moves an interval endpoint from p to j. Need range add suffix [p, j-1] +1. Segment tree natural.

Could use a Binary Indexed Tree over differences for range add and a segment tree for max? No.

Maybe use "segment tree beats"? No.

Maybe there is an O(N) using "max-plus convolution" with monotonic queues? Let's see v_i = P[i] + # {x: last_x > i}. last_x values change. This is similar to maintaining a multiset of last occurrences. v_i = P[i] + count of last occurrences greater than i. If we sort last occurrences, count is number of values with last > i. Since last positions are between 1..j. We need max over i of P[i] + count(last > i). P[i] is nondecreasing step function. count(last > i) is nonincreasing step function. Maybe max occurs at positions just after some last occurrence? Could maintain with a priority queue of candidate i? Let's investigate.

Let L = multiset of last positions of distinct values seen up to j. M(i,j) = #{l in L: l > i}. v_i = P[i] + #{l > i}. P[i] is number of distinct values with first occurrence <= i. Let F = set of first positions. P[i] = #{f in F: f <= i}. So v_i = #{f <= i} + #{l > i}. This is a sum of a prefix count of first positions and suffix count of last positions. For each distinct value, it may have f and l. If a value appears once, f=l. It contributes to v_i if f<=i or l>i. If f=l, then for i<f, l>i true; for i>=f, f<=i true; so always contributes 1. Wait a value appearing once contributes to P[i]+M(i,j) for every i? Let's check value at position k only. If i<k, it's in middle (since middle [i+1,j] includes k) so M counts it. If i>=k, it's in left prefix so P counts it. Thus contributes 1 to v_i for all i. For a value with multiple occurrences, first f and last l. It contributes to P[i] if i>=f; contributes to M if i<l. Yes because if i<l, first condition; if i>=l, then i>=f, second. So every distinct value seen up to j contributes exactly 1 to v_i = P[i]+M(i,j)! Is that true? Earlier I thought duplicates across boundary counted twice, but let's re-evaluate with example [1,1], j=2, i=1: P[1]=1, M(1,2)=1, sum=2. Distinct values seen up to j = {1}, only 1, but sum=2. My per-value contribution logic says value 1 with f=1,l=2: contributes to P if i>=1 yes; contributes to M if i<l=2 yes. It contributes to both, so total 2. This B approach is much simpler! We don't need P, arr, point_set, prefix max. We just maintain B_i = number of values with first occurrence <= i < last occurrence up to j. Initially all 0. For each j from 2 to N-1:

Actually if i>=j, last positions <=j <= i, so interval [first,last-1] doesn't cover i. Our range updates never update i>=j (upper j-1). So B_i=0 for i>=j. We can maintain length N-2 for i=1..N-2. For i>=j, B=0 but if we have indices beyond j-1, they remain 0. Root max over all indices includes zeros and active positive, so maxB correct. No need prefix query. Great!
- Candidate = D[j] + maxB + S[j+1].

This B approach eliminates P entirely, segment tree initialized zeros, no range_max, no point_set. We only need range_add on intervals [prev[j]-1, j-1) when prev[j]>0, and root max. Let's prove B_i formula and update carefully for final explanation (though final code only). But code can be simpler.

Let's derive from scratch to ensure no off-by-one.

For fixed j (middle end), define:
D_j = number of distinct integers in A[1..j].
For a cut i (1<=i<j), let v_i = distinct(1..i) + distinct(i+1..j).
For each value x that appears in A[1..j], let f_x be its first position in A[1..j], l_x be its last position in A[1..j] (<=j). It contributes to distinct(1..i) iff f_x <= i. It contributes to distinct(i+1..j) iff l_x > i? Wait distinct in middle counts x if there is at least one occurrence in (i,j]. Since l_x is last occurrence up to j, if l_x > i then x appears in middle (because the occurrence at l_x is >i and <=j). If l_x <= i, no occurrence in middle. So contribution = I(f_x <= i) + I(l_x > i). For f_x <= l_x:
- If i < f_x: f_x<=i false, l_x>i true (since l_x>=f_x>i) =>1.
- If f_x <= i < l_x: both true =>2.
- If i >= l_x: f_x<=i true, l_x>i false =>1.
Thus contribution = 1 + I(f_x <= i < l_x). Summing over all distinct values in prefix:
v_i = D_j + B_i(j), where B_i(j) = #{x: f_x <= i < l_x}.
Now, as j increases by adding A[j]=x:
- If x is new (prev[j]=0): f_x=l_x=j, so its interval [f,l-1]=[j,j-1] empty; D_j increases by 1; B_i unchanged for all i.
- If x already appeared, let p=prev[j] (previous occurrence). Before update, for value x, last l_old = p, interval [f, p-1]. After update, l_new=j, interval [f, j-1]. Difference in B_i is +1 for i in [p, j-1] (1-based). Because for i<p, both intervals cover if i>=f? Let's check if i<p, before covers if f<=i<p; after covers if f<=i<j; since i<p<j, same. For i>=p, before doesn't cover (i>=p), after covers if i<j. So +1 for p <= i <= j-1. Thus range add +1 to B_i for i in [p, j-1]. In 0-based indices i_cut = i-1, update [p-1, j-2] inclusive = [p-1, j-1) half-open. Good.
Then for any i, total for partition (i,j) = v_i + S[j+1] = D_j + B_i(j) + S[j+1]. Since S[j+1] and D_j independent of i, for fixed j best = D_j + S[j+1] + max_i B_i(j). We can maintain B_i in segment tree with range add and global max. We only need consider i from 1 to N-2 (valid cuts). For i >= j, B_i=0 because no l_x > i? Actually if i>=j, l_x<=j<=i, so interval doesn't cover; B_i=0. For i<j, B_i>=0. So global max over all maintained indices (1..N-2) equals max over valid i? Wait what about i values between j and N-2 are invalid but B=0. If all valid B=0, max=0. If valid B positive, max positive. Since invalid B=0, global max = max(0, max valid B). But max valid B could be 0, so global max = max valid B. Yes. However, what about i=0? Not maintained. We maintain indices 1..N-2. Good.
But wait, for j=2, valid i=1. B_1(2) for A=[3,1] =0. Root max over indices 1..N-2 all 0. Candidate D2=2 +0 + S3=3 =5. Good.
For j=3 sample1, valid i=1,2. B=0. Candidate D3=3+0+S4=2=5. Good.

This B approach means segment tree length should be N-2 (i=1..N-2). Initialize all zeros. Range_add only when prev[j]>0, update [prev[j]-1, j-1) (0-based). Since prev[j]<=j-1, lower <= j-2, upper <= N-2? For j<=N-1, upper j-1 <= N-2, length N-2, good. If prev[j]=0, no update. Root max = seg.d[1]. No need range_max. This is much faster: per j only range_add for repeated values (at most N), and root max O(1). Range_add still O(log), but no second query. Number of range_adds = number of j with prev[j]>0, up to N. ~3e5. Each range_add O(log) with push/update. Total ~114*3e5=34M iterations. Better. Can we avoid segment tree entirely by maintaining max B with a multiset of intervals? B_i = number of intervals covering i. Each update extends an interval from [f,p-1] to [f,j-1], i.e., range add +1 on [p,j-1]. We need max overlap. This is dynamic interval stabbing max. Segment tree natural. But maybe because intervals only extend to the right, we can maintain max overlap with a heap of interval ends and a difference array? To compute max overlap after range add +1 on [p,j-1], we need update many points. Segment tree is fine.

This B approach also avoids computing P entirely. We need prev and Dcur and S. We can compute prev in first pass. We can compute S. Then scan with Dcur and segment tree.

Let's consider if Dcur should be updated before or after B update. For prev[j]>0, Dcur unchanged. For prev[j]==0, Dcur increments. B update none. If we update Dcur before B update, no issue. We'll do:
Dcur = 1
for j in range(2, N):
    p = prev[j]
    if p == 0:
        Dcur += 1
    else:

Now, let's consider if we can further optimize range_add because we only need global max and updates are range add. Could use a segment tree with lazy range add and root max; no range_max. We can implement a simpler lazy segment tree maybe recursive? Iterative still.

Could we implement a segment tree with range add and global max using a "dual" segment tree? Since we only need global max after range adds, maybe we can use a difference array and maintain max prefix? Let's think again. Range add +1 on interval [l,r), global max. This is a classic "range add, global max" segment tree. No simpler.

But maybe because all updates are +1 and intervals [p-1, j-1) with r increasing, we can maintain a Fenwick tree of differences and a max-heap of candidate points? Let's explore for potential O(N log N) with less overhead.

B_i = sum of active intervals covering i. Each interval is [f, l-1] for values with multiple occurrences. When l updates, interval extends to the right. We need max overlap. This is equivalent to maintaining a set of intervals and querying max overlap. There is a known technique: maintain a difference array diff where range add [l,r) +1 is diff[l]+=1, diff[r]-=1. Then B_i = prefix sum of diff up to i. We need max prefix sum of diff. Range add [l,r) updates two points in diff. Query max prefix sum of diff. This is a segment tree over diff supporting point updates and prefix max. Wait! This is important. Instead of range add on B and global max, we can maintain difference array C where C[i] = B_i - B_{i-1} (with B_{-1}=0). Range add +1 on [l,r) corresponds to C[l] +=1, C[r] -=1. Then B_i = sum_{k=0}^i C[k]. We need max over i of prefix sum of C. This is exactly a segment tree over C supporting point updates and global max prefix sum. Point updates are two per range_add, and root stores max prefix sum. This might be faster than range add lazy? Let's see.

We maintain an array C of length M = N-2 (indices 0..M-1), initially all 0. Range add B[l..r-1] +=1 -> C[l] +=1; if r < M, C[r] -=1. If r == M, no need subtract within array? B_i for i>=M? We don't maintain beyond M-1. But B_i for i up to M-1. If r=M, subtract at index M (outside) not needed because prefix sums beyond not queried. However, for prefix sums up to M-1, C[M] not included. So if r<M, C[r]-=1; if r==M, ignore. Then we need max prefix sum of C over indices 0..M-1. A segment tree for point updates and max prefix sum can do this in O(log M) per point update. Range_add [l,r) now becomes two point updates instead of one range_add with lazy. But point update segment tree is simpler and maybe faster: no lazy, just update leaf and recompute nodes. Each range_add requires up to 2 point updates, each O(log), so 2*log per j with p>0, similar to range_add lazy (which is ~6log). Point update segment tree could be much faster! Let's examine.

We need maintain B_i = prefix sum C[0..i]. We need max_i B_i. A segment tree node can store:
- sum: total sum of C in segment
- pref: maximum prefix sum within segment.
For leaf with value c, sum=c, pref=c.
Combine: sum = left.sum + right.sum; pref = max(left.pref, left.sum + right.pref).
Root.pref = max over all prefixes = max_i B_i (including prefix before first? If all negative, root.pref might be negative; but C can be negative due to -1 updates. However B_i nonnegative? Difference array has negative entries, prefix sums nonnegative. Root.pref should be max(0, max B_i) if we include empty prefix? Let's see. If we initialize C all 0, root.pref=0. After updates, B_i >=0. Prefix sum over first k+1 elements. Root.pref as defined max over nonempty prefixes. If all B_i=0, root.pref=0. Good. If C has negative at start? Could C[0] be -1? Range updates [l,r) with l>=0. C[l]+=1, C[r]-=1. C[0] can be -1 if a previous update had l=0? Actually l=0 adds +1, so C[0] not negative from that. But if r=0? r>l>=0, so r>=1. C[r] can be negative. C[0] only gets +1 from updates with l=0, never -1 because r=0 impossible. So prefix sums nonnegative. Root.pref = max B_i. Good.
Point update: C[pos] += delta. Need update leaf sum/pref, then recompute ancestors. No lazy. This is much simpler and faster.

Now, Dcur + maxB + S[j+1]. maxB = root.pref. Need root.pref after point updates. We can maintain segment tree arrays `sum` and `pref` of length 2*size. Initialize all zeros. Point add:
def add(pos, delta):
    k = pos + size
    seg_sum[k] += delta
    seg_pref[k] += delta  # since leaf pref = sum = value
    k >>= 1
    while k:
        left = k<<1; right=left|1
        seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[left] + seg_sum[right]
        # pref = max(pref[left], sum[left] + pref[right])
        lp = seg_pref[left]
        val = seg_sum[left] + seg_pref[right]
        seg_pref[k] = lp if lp >= val else val
        k >>= 1
Need if pos beyond M? We only update pos < M. For r<M, r can equal M? If r<M, pos=r valid 0..M-1. If r==M, no subtract. l always <M because p<=j-1<=N-2? p can be N-2? j=N-1, p<=N-2, l=p-1<=N-3=M-2? Wait M=N-2, l<=N-3=M-1? Actually M=N-2, max l = p-1, p max N-2, l=N-3=M-1? M-1=N-3, yes valid. If p=1, l=0. Good.
What about r = j-1. For j=N-1, r=N-2=M. So r can equal M. If r<M, subtract at r. If r==M, no subtract. Good.

Now, point update segment tree code:
class PrefixMaxSeg:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.size = 1
        while self.size < n:
            self.size <<= 1
        self.sum = [0]*(2*self.size)
        self.pref = [0]*(2*self.size)
    def add(self, pos, delta):
        k = pos + self.size
        self.sum[k] += delta
        self.pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1
        while k:
            lch = k << 1
            rch = lch | 1
            self.sum[k] = self.sum[lch] + self.sum[rch]
            v = self.sum[lch] + self.pref[rch]
            self.pref[k] = self.pref[lch] if self.pref[lch] >= v else v
            k >>= 1
    def max_prefix(self):
        return self.pref[1]

Need if n=0? N>=3 => M=N-2>=1. Good.

Let's test with M=1, size=1, arrays length2 indices0,1. add(0,delta): k=1, sum[1]+=delta,pref[1]+=delta, k>>=1 ->0, while none. max_prefix=pref[1]. Good.

for j in range(2, N):
    p = prev[j]
    if p == 0:
        Dcur += 1
    else:
        l = p - 1
        r = j - 1
        seg.add(l, 1)
        if r < M:
            seg.add(r, -1)
    maxB = seg.max_prefix()
    cand = Dcur + maxB + S[j+1]
    if cand > ans: ans = cand

Let's write code with class:
class PrefixMaxSeg:
    __slots__ = ('size','seg_sum','seg_pref')
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.size = 1
        while self.size < n:
            self.size <<= 1
        self.seg_sum = [0] * (2 * self.size)
        self.seg_pref = [0] * (2 * self.size)
    def add(self, pos, delta):
        k = pos + self.size
        seg_sum = self.seg_sum
        seg_pref = self.seg_pref
        seg_sum[k] += delta
        seg_pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1
        while k:
            left = k << 1
            right = left | 1
            seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[left] + seg_sum[right]
            v = seg_sum[left] + seg_pref[right]
            seg_pref[k] = seg_pref[left] if seg_pref[left] >= v else v
            k >>= 1
    def max_prefix(self):
        return self.seg_pref[1]

Detailed code:

import sys

class PrefixMaxSeg:
    __slots__ = ('size', 'seg_sum', 'seg_pref')
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.size = 1
        while self.size < n:
            self.size <<= 1
        self.seg_sum = [0] * (2 * self.size)
        self.seg_pref = [0] * (2 * self.size)

    def add(self, pos, delta):
        k = pos + self.size
        seg_sum = self.seg_sum
        seg_pref = self.seg_pref
        seg_sum[k] += delta
        seg_pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1
        while k:
            left = k << 1
            right = left | 1
            seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[left] + seg_sum[right]
            v = seg_sum[left] + seg_pref[right]
            seg_pref[k] = seg_pref[left] if seg_pref[left] >= v else v
            k >>= 1

    def max_prefix(self):
        return self.seg_pref[1]

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]
    # del data? A references ints; data list can be deleted to save memory.
    # But if we del data, A still holds references to int objects? A = data[1:] creates new list of references. Yes.
    del data
    last = [0] * (N + 1)
    prev = [0] * (N + 1)
    for pos, a in enumerate(A, 1):
        p = last[a]
        prev[pos] = p
        last[a] = pos
    del last

    S = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for pos in range(N, 0, -1):
        a = A[pos - 1]
        if not seen[a]:
            seen[a] = True
            cnt += 1
        S[pos] = cnt
    del seen, A

    M = N - 2
    seg = PrefixMaxSeg(M)
    Dcur = 1
    ans = 0
    for j in range(2, N):
        p = prev[j]
        if p == 0:
            Dcur += 1
        else:
            l = p - 1
            r = j - 1
            seg.add(l, 1)
            if r < M:
                seg.add(r, -1)
        maxB = seg.max_prefix()
        cand = Dcur + maxB + S[j + 1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if `B_i` can be maintained by difference array with intervals [f, l-1] where f and l are first/last up to current j. When l updates, interval extends. Our range add [p, j-1] is exactly the set of i where the interval coverage changes from 0 to 1? Let's verify for i in [p, j-1] 1-based:
Before update, value x's last l_old=p. It contributes to B_i if f <= i < p.
After update, l_new=j. Contributes if f <= i < j.
For i < p: before condition f<=i<p; after f<=i<j. Since i<p, both same (if f<=i then both true; else both false). No change.
For i >= p and i < j: before false (i>=p), after true (i<j and i>=p>=f? Wait need f<=i. Since p is an occurrence of x, f<=p<=i, so f<=i. Thus after true). Change +1.
For i >= j: before false, after false. No change.
So update [p, j-1] inclusive. Good.

Let's maybe derive root.pref with padded zeros in more detail:
We have array C of length M. We pad with zeros to size S. The segment tree stores for each segment:
- sum = sum of C in segment
- pref = maximum sum of a non-empty prefix of this segment? Or can be empty? We set leaf pref = value, so non-empty prefix. For zero leaves, pref=0. Combine pref = max(left.pref, left.sum + right.pref). This gives maximum sum over a non-empty prefix of the concatenated segment. For a segment of all zeros, pref=0. Root.pref = max over non-empty prefixes of the padded array. Non-empty prefixes of padded array correspond to prefixes of C of length 1..M (plus extra zeros). The prefix sum after all M real elements plus k zeros is total_sum = B_{M-1}. This is included as a prefix of length M (if M>0) and also longer prefixes with same sum. So root.pref = max_{1<=t<=M} B_{t-1} (and possibly B_{M-1} repeated). Since B_i>=0 and B_0>=0, this equals max_i B_i. Good. If M=0 not possible. If B_i all 0, root.pref=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if leaf pref for negative value should be negative, but for a segment with left sum positive, right pref negative, combine may choose left.pref. Good.

Now, let's consider if `seg_pref` for internal nodes initially 0; if we never update some nodes, they remain 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if point add delta=-1 at pos r where r is a padded zero? We only if r<M, so real. Good.

Now, let's consider if we need to update `seg_pref` for leaf with `delta` but if leaf value was 0 and delta=-1, pref=-1. Good.

import sys

class PrefixMaxSeg:
    ...
def main():
    data = ...
    ...
    # M is number of possible first cut positions i=1..N-2 (0-based)
    M = N - 2
    seg = PrefixMaxSeg(M)
    pref_distinct = 1  # D_1
    ans = 0
    for j in range(2, N):
        p = prev[j]
        if p == 0:
            pref_distinct += 1
        else:
            l = p - 1
            r = j - 1
            seg.add(l, 1)
            if r < M:
                seg.add(r, -1)
        max_extra = seg.max_prefix()
        cand = pref_distinct + max_extra + S[j+1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand
    print(ans)

Let's attempt to prove the algorithm in a concise way (not final, but for my confidence):
For each j, D_j = # distinct in A[1..j]. For cut i, left+middle distinct count = D_j + E_i, where E_i = # values with first occurrence in [1..i] and last occurrence in [i+1..j] (i.e., appearing on both sides of cut i within prefix). Because each distinct value in prefix contributes at least1 to left+middle; values spanning the cut contribute one extra. E_i is exactly B_i. As j increases:
- New value: D_j increments, E unchanged.
- Repeated value with previous occurrence p: D_j unchanged, E_i increases by1 exactly for p <= i <= j-1. This is range add.
We maintain E_i via difference array: range add [p-1, j-1) -> C[p-1]++, C[j-1]-- (if within). Segment tree stores prefix sums of C and their max. Then answer = max_j D_j + max_i E_i + S[j+1]. This is correct.

So our segment tree index

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys


class PrefixMaxSeg:
    __slots__ = ("size", "seg_sum", "seg_pref")

    def __init__(self, n):
        self.size = 1
        while self.size < n:
            self.size <<= 1
        self.seg_sum = [0] * (2 * self.size)
        self.seg_pref = [0] * (2 * self.size)

    def add(self, pos, delta):
        k = pos + self.size
        seg_sum = self.seg_sum
        seg_pref = self.seg_pref

        seg_sum[k] += delta
        seg_pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1

        while k:
            left = k << 1
            right = left | 1
            seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[left] + seg_sum[right]
            v = seg_sum[left] + seg_pref[right]
            seg_pref[k] = seg_pref[left] if seg_pref[left] >= v else v
            k >>= 1

    def max_prefix(self):
        return self.seg_pref[1]


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]
    del data

    prev = [0] * (N + 1)
    last = [0] * (N + 1)

    for pos, a in enumerate(A, 1):
        p = last[a]
        prev[pos] = p
        last[a] = pos

    del last

    suffix = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0

    for pos in range(N, 0, -1):
        a = A[pos - 1]
        if not seen[a]:
            seen[a] = True
            cnt += 1
        suffix[pos] = cnt

    del seen, A

    # Indices 0..N-3 correspond to first cuts i = 1..N-2.
    M = N - 2
    seg = PrefixMaxSeg(M)

    distinct_prefix = 1  # distinct count in A[1..1]
    ans = 0

    for j in range(2, N):
        p = prev[j]

        if p == 0:
            distinct_prefix += 1
        else:
            # Extra overlap count increases for cuts i in [p, j-1].
            # In 0-based index: [p-1, j-1)
            l = p - 1
            r = j - 1

            seg.add(l, 1)
            if r < M:
                seg.add(r, -1)

        max_extra = seg.max_prefix()
        cand = distinct_prefix + max_extra + suffix[j + 1]

        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
